        \addlegendentry{$\ket{11}$: $\sqrt2\Omega$}
        \draw[dashed,color=subtle] (axis cs:4.293,0) -- (axis cs:4.293,1.0);
        \node[font=\footnotesize,color=subtle,anchor=south] at (axis cs:4.293,0.94) {phase jump $\xi$};
      \end{axis}
      \end{tikzpicture}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.44\textwidth}
      Levine et al.\ (2019): two pulses of length $\tau$ at detuning $\Delta$, the second with its laser phase jumped by $\xi$.
      \vskip1mm
      Each pulse is one full detuned cycle for $\ket{11}$; the phase jump steers $\ket{01}$ back. Both return, and $\phi_{11}=2\phi_{01}+\pi$: a CZ.
      \vskip1mm
      Solving the three conditions: $\Delta/\Omega=0.377$, $\Omega\tau=4.293$, $\xi=3.902$.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \begin{takeaway}One global pulse entangles every blockaded pair in parallel. A three-atom pulse gives a native CCZ.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Neutral atoms: records, and atoms that move}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \begin{greyblock}
        \blocktitle{Fidelity}
        Time-optimal pulse (Jandura--Pupillo):\par
        \textbf{99.5\%} on 60 $^{87}$Rb atoms in parallel, 0.26\,$\mu$s (Evered et al., 2023).\par
        \textbf{99.85\%} (Evered et al., 2026).
      \end{greyblock}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \begin{greyblock}
        \blocktitle{Architecture}
        Tweezers \emph{move} atoms mid-computation: the non-local links qLDPC codes need. Many errors are detectable \emph{erasures}.
      \end{greyblock}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip2mm
  Main error: decay from $\ket r$ during the gate, $\sim10^{-3}$. Moves and imaging make the cycle $\approx1$\,ms.
  \begin{takeaway}That 1\,ms is the clock of the Oratomic blueprint (Act XI): $10^4$ atoms, days to months.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Spins in silicon: exchange from tunnelling}
  \begin{columns}[c]
    \begin{column}{0.42\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[x=10mm,y=10mm,font=\small]
        \draw[very thick,color=body] (0,2.2) -- (0.4,2.2) .. controls (0.7,2.2) and (0.8,0) .. (1.3,0) .. controls (1.8,0) and (1.9,1.3) .. (2.2,1.3) .. controls (2.5,1.3) and (2.6,0) .. (3.1,0) .. controls (3.6,0) and (3.7,2.2) .. (4.0,2.2) -- (4.4,2.2);
        \node[circle,fill=cblue,inner sep=2.5pt] at (1.3,0.35) {}; \draw[-{Stealth[length=1.6mm]},thick,color=cblue] (1.3,0.25) -- (1.3,0.75);
        \node[circle,fill=cblue,inner sep=2.5pt] at (3.1,0.35) {}; \draw[-{Stealth[length=1.6mm]},thick,color=cblue] (3.1,0.75) -- (3.1,0.25);
        \draw[<->,thick,color=cverm] (1.45,0.95) to[bend left=50] (2.95,0.95);
        \node[color=cverm] at (2.2,2.0) {$t_c$};
        \node[color=subtle,font=\footnotesize] at (2.2,2.55) {barrier gate};
        \node[font=\footnotesize] at (1.3,-0.35) {dot 1}; \node[font=\footnotesize] at (3.1,-0.35) {dot 2};
      \end{tikzpicture}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.54\textwidth}
      Only the singlet can hop virtually (cost $U$). Schrieffer--Wolff:
      \[ H=J\big(\vec S_1\!\cdot\vec S_2-\tfrac14\big),\quad J=\frac{4t_c^2}{U}. \]
      \textbf{Equal Zeeman:} SWAP at $Jt=\pi$.\par
      \textbf{Zeeman gradient $\gg J$:} only $\frac J4Z_1Z_2$ survives: \textbf{CZ} at $\int J=\pi$.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip2mm
  {\small CZ 99.5--99.65\% (2022); 99.04--99.56\% from a 300\,mm industrial foundry (2025).}
  \begin{takeaway}CMOS manufacturing and $\sim$100\,nm pitch; the challenge is device variability.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Photons: linear optics is a small Lie algebra}
  \begin{columns}[c]
    \begin{column}{0.58\textwidth}
      Beam splitters and phase shifters, $H=\sum h_{ij}a_i^\dagger a_j$:
      \vskip1mm
      \textbf{single-qubit gates are exact} (one photon in two modes);
      \vskip1mm
      \textbf{entangling gates are not}: the algebra is $\mathfrak u(m)$, dimension $m^2$, but $N$ photons span $\binom{m+N-1}{N}$ dimensions;
      \vskip1mm
      \textbf{measurement is the way out}: interference plus detection (KLM, 2001); today \emph{fusion}, a Bell measurement.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.38\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[x=9mm,y=9mm,font=\small]
        \draw[thick,color=cblue,-{Stealth[length=2mm]}] (0,0) -- (1.45,1.45); \draw[thick,color=cblue,-{Stealth[length=2mm]}] (0,3) -- (1.45,1.55);
        \draw[thick,color=cpurple,-{Stealth[length=2mm]}] (1.55,1.55) -- (3,3); \draw[thick,color=cpurple,-{Stealth[length=2mm]}] (1.55,1.45) -- (3,0);
        \draw[line width=2pt,color=body] (1.5,0.9) -- (1.5,2.1);
        \node[anchor=east] at (0,0) {$\ket1$}; \node[anchor=east] at (0,3) {$\ket1$};
        \node[anchor=west] at (3,3) {$2$ or $0$}; \node[anchor=west] at (3,0) {$0$ or $2$};
      \end{tikzpicture}\par
      {\small Hong--Ou--Mandel: the photons always leave together}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \begin{takeaway}PsiQuantum (2025): fusion 99.22\%, chip-to-chip 99.72\%. Photon loss is the main error.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Cat qubits: one kind of error switched off}
  \begin{columns}[c]
    \begin{column}{0.56\textwidth}
      Kerr resonator with a two-photon drive:
      \[ H=-K\big(a^{\dagger2}-\alpha^2\big)\big(a^2-\alpha^2\big). \]
      Ground states $\ket{\pm\alpha}$: the qubit.
      \vskip1mm
      \textbf{Bit flip}: tunnelling, $\propto e^{-2|\alpha|^2}$ (over 10\,s measured).\par
      \textbf{Phase flip}: photon loss, only $\propto|\alpha|^2$.
      \vskip1mm
      So a repetition code suffices. AWS (2025): distance 5, 1.65\% logical error per cycle.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.4\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[x=9mm,y=9mm,font=\small]
        \draw[-{Stealth[length=2mm]},color=subtle] (-2.6,0) -- (2.6,0) node[right] {$x$};
        \draw[-{Stealth[length=2mm]},color=subtle] (0,-1.6) -- (0,1.6) node[right] {$p$};
        \shade[inner color=cblue!70,outer color=white] (1.5,0) circle (0.7);
        \shade[inner color=cverm!70,outer color=white] (-1.5,0) circle (0.7);
        \node at (1.5,1.0) {$\ket{\alpha}$}; \node at (-1.5,1.0) {$\ket{-\alpha}$};
        \draw[<->,thick,dashed,color=body] (-1.3,-0.8) to[bend right=35] (1.3,-0.8);
        \node[font=\footnotesize,fill=white,inner sep=1pt] at (0,-1.5) {bit flip: rare};
      \end{tikzpicture}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \begin{takeaway}Biased noise underlies the 13\,436-qubit RSA and 126\,133-qubit ECDLP cat-qubit estimates.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Where the error rate $p$ comes from}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \blocktitle{Decoherence during the gate}
      With relaxation time $T_1$ and dephasing time $T_\phi$, a gate of length $t$ costs at least
      \[ 1-F\approx\frac t3\Big(\frac1{T_1}+\frac1{T_\phi}\Big) . \]
      {\small 40\,ns CZ, $T_1=70\,\mu$s, $T_\phi=150\,\mu$s: $5.6\times10^{-4}$ for two qubits, a sixth of Willow's 0.33\%.}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \blocktitle{Everything else}
      \textbf{Control errors}: an over-rotation $\delta$ costs $\delta^2/6$, but adds in \emph{amplitude}; random Pauli frames make it stochastic.\par
      \textbf{Leakage, crosstalk, loss}: not Pauli errors; detecting them as \emph{erasures} raises the error rate the surface code tolerates from 0.94\% to 4.15\% (Wu et al.\ 2022).
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \begin{takeaway}The ratio of coherence time to gate time is each platform's basic figure of merit. Today most of the error is control, not coherence.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Six technologies, one table}
  \centering\small
  \setlength{\tabcolsep}{5pt}
  \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
  \begin{tabular}{@{}lllll@{}}
    \toprule
    platform & native gate & 2q gate time & best 2q error & QEC cycle \\
    \midrule
    transmons & CZ, fSim & 20--60\,ns & 0.1\%; 0.33\% on 105 & 1.1\,$\mu$s \\
    \quad fixed freq. & ECR (cross-res.) & $\sim$0.5\,$\mu$s & 0.74\% median & $\sim$1\,$\mu$s \\
    \quad fluxonium & CZ, CNOT & 60\,ns & 0.06\% & -- \\
    trapped ions & MS, $ZZ(\theta)$ & 10--200\,$\mu$s & 0.008\%; 0.08\% on 98 & ms \\
    neutral atoms & CZ, CCZ (blockade) & 0.2--0.5\,$\mu$s & 0.15\% & $\sim$1\,ms \\
    silicon spins & CZ, $\sqrt{\mathrm{SWAP}}$ (exchange) & $\sim$100\,ns & 0.35\% & -- \\
    photons & fusion (Bell measurement) & ns & 0.78\% & -- \\
    cat qubits & bias-preserving CX & -- & 1.65\% logical, $d{=}5$ & -- \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}\par
  \vskip1mm
  {\scriptsize\color{subtle}as reported, with different benchmarking protocols; best results are often on two qubits, not a full processor}
  \begin{takeaway}Modulo single-qubit gates, nearly all make the same gate: the CNOT point.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{What the physics means for Shor}
  \begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{2mm}
    \item \textbf{The cost model is the same on every platform.} $T$ and even CCZ are physically cheap, but none of that survives encoding: logical $T$ and Toffoli still need magic states (Act II). Shor costs its logical Toffoli count everywhere.
    \item \textbf{The platform enters through four numbers:} the error rate $p$ (qubits per logical qubit), the cycle time (time per Toffoli), the connectivity (whether qLDPC codes are possible) and the parallelism.
    \item \textbf{Superconducting qubits are fast and local; ions are accurate, connected and $10^3\times$ slower; atoms move and parallelise, at a slow cycle.} That is exactly the spread of the blueprints in Act XI.
  \end{enumerate}
  \resolved{Every gate is an effective Hamiltonian switched on for a time. Universality is a Lie-bracket condition, and each technology's entangling gate is a different route to the same point of the Weyl chamber.}
\end{frame}

% =====================================================================================
\act{Act XI --- the 2026 frontier, and the machines that would run it}

\begin{frame}{Act XI --- where the circuits of this deck sit today}
  \begin{difficulty}{11}The textbook circuits are the floor. Where is the ceiling, and what does a machine that runs it look like?\end{difficulty}
  \vskip5mm
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.53\textwidth}
      \blocktitle{Four things changed in 2025--26}
      \begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{1mm}
        \item \textbf{Arithmetic}: ECDLP-256 from $1.3\times10^{11}$ to $4$--$9\times10^7$ Toffolis.
        \item \textbf{Magic states}: $10\times$ cheaper to make.
        \item \textbf{New codes}: $\approx25$ physical qubits per logical, not $\approx1\,000$.
        \item \textbf{Blueprints} from circuit to hardware: Google, Pinnacle, Oratomic, IonQ.
      \end{enumerate}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.43\textwidth}
      \begin{greyblock}
        \blocktitle{Three slides of vocabulary first}
        Acts II--IX counted \emph{logical} qubits and Toffolis; Act X showed the physical gates. To talk about machines we need the words for codes and clocks. Nothing beyond those three slides is assumed.
      \end{greyblock}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Vocabulary I --- logical qubits are made of physical ones}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \begin{greyblock}
        \blocktitle{Code, distance, threshold}
        A \textbf{code} $[[n,k,d]]$ stores $k$ \emph{logical} qubits in $n$ noisy \emph{physical} ones; errors on fewer than $d$ qubits are caught. Below a \textbf{threshold} error rate ($\approx1\%$) the logical error falls exponentially in $d$. Standard assumption: $p=10^{-3}$.
      \end{greyblock}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \begin{greyblock}
        \blocktitle{Surface code}
        One logical qubit per patch of $2d^2-1$ physical qubits on a square grid, nearest-neighbour gates only. A $10^{10}$-Toffoli run needs $d\approx27$: $\approx1\,500$ physical qubits per logical one.
      \end{greyblock}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \begin{takeaway}Physical qubits $\approx$ logical qubits $\times$ (physical per logical) $+$ factories.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Vocabulary II --- clocks, decoders and factories}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \blocktitle{Cycle, decoder, reaction time}
      Each \textbf{cycle} ($1\,\mu$s superconducting, $\sim$1 ms atoms and ions) measures all checks; a classical \textbf{decoder} infers the error. The \textbf{reaction time} ($\approx10\,\mu$s) is decoding plus control latency: every non-Clifford gate waits for it.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \blocktitle{Magic-state factories}
      Blocks of physical qubits that mass-produce the $|T\rangle$ states of Act II: by \emph{distillation} (15 noisy copies $\to$ 1) or, since 2024, \emph{cultivation} (one copy grown in a single patch, $\approx10\times$ cheaper).
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip2mm
  \blocktitle{Lattice surgery}
  How logical operations are done: merge two blocks, measure their joint parity for $\approx d$ cycles, split. A Toffoli consumes a magic state through a few of these.
  \begin{takeaway}Runtime $\approx$ Toffolis $\times$ time per Toffoli.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Vocabulary III --- denser codes}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.52\textwidth}
      \blocktitle{qLDPC codes}
      The surface code's $\approx2d^2$ physical per logical is forced on any code whose checks are local on a 2D grid. \textbf{qLDPC} (quantum low-density parity-check) codes keep every check small but let it reach across the chip. IBM's \emph{gross code} $[[144,12,12]]$: 12 logical qubits in 288 physical, against $\approx3\,000$ on surface codes; the \emph{lifted-product} codes of the atom and ion designs reach 3--5 per logical.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.44\textwidth}
      \blocktitle{Yoked surface codes}
      A half-way house: one extra parity check across a row of patches, $\approx3\times$ denser, still local.
      \vskip2mm
      \blocktitle{The price of density}
      Long-range connections (couplers, or moving atoms and ions); qubits sharing a block are accessed partly one at a time; decoding is harder.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \begin{takeaway}\textbf{Pareto frontier}: the estimates no other beats on \emph{both} axes (qubits and Toffolis, or qubits and time).\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{ECDLP-256: a competition on one point addition}
  \begin{columns}[c]
    \begin{column}{0.58\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}
      \begin{axis}[scale only axis,width=0.8\linewidth,height=3.7cm,xmode=log,ymode=log,xmin=700,xmax=3200,ymin=7e5,ymax=1.2e7,
        xlabel={logical qubits},ylabel={Toffolis per point addition},xtick={800,1000,1500,2000,3000},xticklabels={800,1000,1500,2000,3000},
        grid=both,clip mode=individual,
        legend style={at={(0.5,-0.27)},anchor=north,legend columns=2,/tikz/every even column/.append style={column sep=4pt}}]
        \addplot[very thick,cpurple,forget plot] coordinates {(825,1.2e7) (825,8.8e6) (1151,8.8e6) (1151,1.299e6) (1321,1.299e6) (1321,9.53e5) (3200,9.53e5)};
        \addplot[dashed,subtle,domain=700:3200,samples=2,forget plot] {1.5e9/x};
        \addplot[dashed,subtle,domain=700:3200,samples=2,forget plot] {3e9/x};
        \addplot[only marks,mark=square,mark size=2.6pt,subtle,thick] coordinates {(2715,3.96e6)};\addlegendentry{ECDSA.fail baseline}
        \addplot[only marks,mark=*,mark size=2.6pt,cverm] coordinates {(1175,2.7e6) (1425,2.1e6)};\addlegendentry{Google (zk-attested)}
        \addplot[only marks,mark=square*,mark size=2.6pt,cblue] coordinates {(1192,2.39e6) (1446,1.86e6)};\addlegendentry{Schrottenloher (open)}
        \addplot[only marks,mark=diamond*,mark size=3.4pt,cpurple] coordinates {(1151,1.299e6) (825,8.8e6) (1321,9.53e5)};\addlegendentry{ECDSA.fail best}
        \node[font=\scriptsize,cpurple,anchor=north west] at (axis cs:850,8.3e6) {825 / 8.8M};
        \node[font=\scriptsize,cpurple,anchor=east,fill=white,inner sep=1pt] at (axis cs:1110,1.3e6) {1\,151 / 1.30M};
        \node[font=\scriptsize,cpurple,anchor=north west,fill=white,inner sep=1pt] at (axis cs:1350,9.0e5) {1\,321 / 0.95M};
        \node[font=\scriptsize,subtle,anchor=south west,inner sep=1pt] at (axis cs:715,4.3e6) {$Q\!\cdot\!T=3$B};
        \node[font=\scriptsize,subtle,anchor=south west,inner sep=1pt] at (axis cs:715,2.15e6) {1.5B};
      \end{axis}
      \end{tikzpicture}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.38\textwidth}
      \small
      \textbf{March 2026.} Google: $\le1\,200$ qubits / $\le90$M Toffolis for secp256k1, published as a \emph{zero-knowledge proof} that such a circuit exists, not as a circuit.\par
      \textbf{June.} Schrottenloher: open circuits, same counts.\par
      \textbf{May--Sep.} \emph{ECDSA.fail}: a public leaderboard scored by qubits $\times$ Toffolis. 100+ people and their AI agents halve Google's score; the purple staircase is its frontier.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

% ------------------------------------------------------------------ inside the 1,151-qubit entry
\begin{frame}{Inside the leaderboard's 1\,151-qubit entry}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.52\textwidth}
      \small ECDSA.fail scores one circuit: the in-place mixed addition $R\mapsto R+A$, with the addend $A=(x_A,y_A)$ given as classical bits and materialised only while it is used.
      \vskip2mm
      Submission \texttt{8e9c9a2} runs the seven updates of Act VIII as six stages, each specialised to secp256k1 and to a classical addend. The next slides follow it stage by stage.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.44\textwidth}
      \begin{peachblock}[fontupper=\small]\peachtitle{As evaluated by the challenge}
        $Q=1\,151$ peak logical qubits\\
        $T=1\,299\,453$ executed Toffolis (average)\\
        score $Q\times T=1\,495\,670\,403$
      \end{peachblock}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip2mm
  \begin{greyblock}[fontupper=\small]
    \textbf{Not in the submission:} the scalar registers, the Fourier transforms, and the coherent lookup of $A$. A full Shor circuit needs a validated lookup--use--unlookup around it: in this repo's build, three loads and one merged repair, $197\,364$ Toffolis per addition at $w=16$ (Act XII).
  \end{greyblock}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Six stages, six optimisation stacks}
  \centering\footnotesize
  \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.04}
  \begin{tabular}{@{}l>{\raggedright}p{31mm}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{84mm}@{}}
    \toprule
    stage & update & optimisation stack \\
    \midrule
    1.\ differences & $X\mathrel{-}=x_A$, \ $Y\mathrel{-}=y_A$ & addend loaded off-peak into one serialised register; 53-bit fold; 19-bit comparison; carries vented \\
    2.\ division & $Y\leftarrow d_y/d_x=\lambda$ & Jump-2 walk; base-5 dialog codec; streamed replay; shrinking widths; narrowed comparisons \\
    3.\ prepare $X$ & $X\mathrel{+}=3x_A$ & $3x_A\bmod p$ computed classically; transient copy; 53-bit fold; 19-bit comparison \\
    4.\ square & $X\mathrel{-}=\lambda^2$ & three-square Karatsuba; symmetric squaring; streamed products; pseudo-Mersenne fold \\
    5.\ multiply & $Y\leftarrow\lambda X$ & the Jump-2 engine again, with a fresh dialog, run as division backwards \\
    6.\ output & $Y\mathrel{-}=y_A$, \ $X\leftarrow x_A-X$ & fused complement-plus-add; carries cleared by measurement; 53-bit fold \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}\par
  \vskip1mm
  \raggedright{\small Three kinds of saving recur: \textbf{liveness} (nothing held longer than needed), \textbf{specialisation} to $p=2^{256}-2^{32}-977$, and \textbf{measurement} in place of reverse gates.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 1: coordinate differences through one transient register}
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
    \foreach \y/\r/\a/\c/\o/\al in {14/X/x_R/x_A/d_x/{allocate $C$\\256 qubits},0/Y/y_R/y_A/d_y/{reallocate $C$\\same 256 slots}} {
      \node[lab,font=\small] at (13,\y) {$\r:|\a\rangle$};
      \node[bxl,minimum width=22mm,minimum height=9mm] (a\r) at (27,\y) {\al};
      \node[bxl,minimum width=22mm,minimum height=9mm] (b\r) at (54,\y) {load fixed bits\\$C\leftarrow \c$};
      \node[bxa,minimum width=24mm,minimum height=9mm] (c\r) at (82,\y) {$\r\leftarrow \r-C\pmod p$\\vented subtraction};
      \node[bxl,minimum width=22mm,minimum height=9mm] (d\r) at (110,\y) {unload $\c$\\free $C$};
      \node[bxk,minimum width=13mm,minimum height=9mm] (e\r) at (134,\y) {$\r=|\o\rangle$};
      \draw[flow] (14,\y) -- (a\r); \draw[flow] (a\r) -- (b\r); \draw[flow] (b\r) -- (c\r); \draw[flow] (c\r) -- (d\r); \draw[flow] (d\r) -- (e\r);}
  \end{tikzpicture}\par
  \vskip2mm
  $|x_R\rangle_X|y_R\rangle_Y|0\rangle_C\ \longmapsto\ |d_x\rangle_X|d_y\rangle_Y|0\rangle_C$\par
  \vskip2mm
  \raggedright
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \small \textbf{Serialised.} The two subtractions are independent, so they share one 256-qubit register in turn: only one coordinate copy is ever live.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \small \textbf{A classical bit is an $X^{c_i}$.} Loading applies $X$ where $c_i=1$; unloading repeats it, since $(X^{c_i})^2=I$. No quantum input, no Toffoli.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 1 detail: subtract, then fix one borrow}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.52\textwidth}
      \small Subtracting on 256 bits leaves a final borrow $b=[y<x]$:
      \[ r_0=(y-x)\bmod 2^{256}=\begin{cases} y-x, & y\ge x,\\ 2^{256}+y-x, & y<x.\end{cases} \]
      With $p=2^{256}-F$, $F=2^{32}+977$: $\ 2^{256}\equiv F\pmod p$, so
      \[ (y-x)\bmod p = r_0-b\,F \pmod{2^{256}} , \]
      a borrow-controlled constant, not a comparison against $p$.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.44\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
        \draw[draw=rulegrey,fill=white] (0,0) rectangle (56,6);
        \draw[draw=cpurple,fill=cpurple!18] (0,0) rectangle (4.2,6);
        \draw[draw=cverm,fill=cverm!16] (44.4,0) rectangle (56,6);
        \node[font=\scriptsize,color=subtle,anchor=south west] at (0,6.3) {bit 255};
        \node[font=\scriptsize,color=subtle,anchor=south east] at (56,6.3) {bit 0};
        \draw[decorate,decoration={brace,amplitude=3pt,mirror},thick,color=cpurple] (0,-1) -- (4.2,-1) node[midway,below=3pt,font=\scriptsize,align=center] {top 19:\\clear $b$};
        \draw[decorate,decoration={brace,amplitude=3pt,mirror},thick,color=cverm] (44.4,-1) -- (56,-1) node[midway,below=3pt,font=\scriptsize,align=center] {low 53:\\$-bF$};
      \end{tikzpicture}\par
      \vskip2mm
      \raggedright\small The correction touches only the low 53 bits, and $b$ is uncomputed by a phase test on the top 19.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip2mm
  {\small IonQ's adder in Act XII makes the same move ($\kappa=65$, $\delta=32$). The truncations are validated on the benchmark's inputs, not proven for all.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 1 detail: venting a carry instead of uncomputing it}
  \begin{columns}[c]
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
        \foreach \y/\l in {16/q_1,9/q_2,0/{|0\rangle}} {\node[lab,font=\small] at (3,\y) {$\l$}; \draw[wire] (4,\y) -- (62,\y);}
        \draw[thick,color=body] (12,16) -- (12,0); \node[dot] at (12,16) {}; \node[dot] at (12,9) {}; \oplusat{12}{0}
        \node[note] at (12,-5) {$c=q_1q_2$};
        \node[bxa,minimum width=13mm,minimum height=5mm] at (25,0) {use $c$};
        \node[hop,minimum size=4.5mm] at (36,0) {$H$};
        \node[meter,minimum size=5mm] (m) at (44,0) {$X$};
        \draw[clw] (m.east) -- (54,0) -- (54,8);
        \draw[thick,color=body] (54,16) -- (54,9); \node[dot] at (54,16) {}; \node[dot] at (54,9) {};
        \node[note] at (54,-5) {CZ if $m=1$};
      \end{tikzpicture}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \small
      \[ \textstyle\sum\alpha_{q_1q_2}|q_1q_2\rangle|0\rangle\mapsto\sum\alpha_{q_1q_2}|q_1q_2\rangle|q_1q_2\rangle \]
      An $X$-basis measurement with outcome $m$ leaves only a phase:
      \[ \textstyle\sum\alpha_{q_1q_2}(-1)^{m\,q_1q_2}|q_1q_2\rangle , \]
      and $\mathrm{CZ}^m$ removes it exactly: no reverse Toffoli.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip3mm
  {\small Selected internal borrows are vented this way; the final borrow stays live through $-bF$ and is cleared by the phase test. It is Act VII's temporary AND applied to a carry chain, and the idea Gidney's classical--quantum adder (Act XII) uses on every carry.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 2: the slope by dialog-GCD division}
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
    \draw[wire] (16,26) -- (142,26); \node[lab,font=\small] at (15.5,26) {$X:|d_x\rangle$};
    \draw[twire] (16,11) -- (142,11); \node[lab,font=\small,color=cverm] at (15.5,11) {$Y:|d_y\rangle$};
    \draw[wire] (16,0) -- (142,0); \node[lab,font=\small] at (15.5,0) {$Z:|0\rangle$};
    \node[bxl,minimum width=18mm,minimum height=5mm] (ini) at (34,37) {initialise $u\leftarrow p$};
    \node[bxo,minimum width=27mm,minimum height=11mm] (walk) at (34,26) {forward Jump-2 walk\\$(u,v)\leftarrow(p,d_x)$\\halve, swap, subtract};
    \draw[thick,color=body] (ini) -- (walk);
    \node[bxl,minimum width=26mm,minimum height=9mm] (tau) at (68,17.5) {base-5 dialog codec\\$\tau$: compressed transcript};
    \node[bxk,minimum width=27mm,minimum height=11mm] (rev) at (126,26) {reverse the walk\\decode $\tau$, restore $X$\\clear $(u,v)$};
    \draw[flow] (walk.south east) -- (tau.west); \draw[flow] (tau.east) -- (rev.south west);
    \node[bxo,minimum width=27mm,minimum height=15mm] (bez) at (34,5.5) {prepare the Bézout pair\\$(Y,Z):(d_y,0)\mapsto(0,d_y)$};
    \node[bxa,minimum width=31mm,minimum height=15mm] (it) at (100,5.5) {inverse-transpose steps\\$|0\rangle_Y|d_y\rangle_Z\mapsto|\lambda\rangle_Y|0\rangle_Z$};
    \draw[flow] (walk.east) ++(0,2) -- (92,28) -- (92,13.2);
    \node[note,anchor=south] at (71,28) {live step controls};
    \node[note,anchor=south east] at (142,26.4) {};
    \node[note,anchor=south east] at (142,11.4) {$Y=|\lambda\rangle$};
    \node[note,anchor=south east] at (142,0.4) {$Z$ released};
  \end{tikzpicture}\par
  \vskip2mm
  $|p\rangle_u|d_x\rangle_X|0\rangle_Y|d_y\rangle_Z\ \longmapsto\ |0\rangle_u|d_x\rangle_X|\lambda\rangle_Y|0\rangle_Z,\qquad \lambda=d_y\,d_x^{-1}\bmod p$\par
  \vskip1mm
  \raggedright{\small The walk drives $(u,v)$ from $(p,d_x)$ towards $(1,0)$ and temporarily modifies $X$; the reverse walk restores it. $(Y,Z)$ is the coefficient pair, carried along by the same decisions.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 2 detail: one Jump-2 step}
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
    \foreach \i/\c/\t in {0/bxo/{\textbf{active state}\\$u$ odd\\$v\ge0$},1/bxa/{\textbf{first halving}\\$v\leftarrow v/2$},2/bxa/{\textbf{second halving?}\\$s_2=[v\text{ even}]$\\$v\leftarrow v/2$ if $s_2$},3/bxl/{\textbf{branch, order}\\$b=v_0$\\$s=b\wedge[v<u]$\\swap if $s$},4/bxl/{\textbf{subtract}\\$v\leftarrow v-u$\\if $b$},5/bxk/{\textbf{record}\\$\sigma=(b,s,s_2)$}} {
      \node[style=\c,text width=18.5mm,minimum height=16mm] (s\i) at (\i*24.2,0) {\t};}
    \foreach \i/\j in {0/1,1/2,2/3,3/4,4/5} \draw[flow] (s\i) -- (s\j);
  \end{tikzpicture}\par
  \vskip1mm
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \begin{panel}[equal height group=j2]
        \blocktitle{Jump-2 in one equation}
        \small \[ v\longmapsto\begin{cases} v/2, & v/2 \text{ odd},\\ v/4, & v/2 \text{ even}, \end{cases} \]
        then order and subtract. Two halvings per symbol at most: \textbf{261} steps, where Act VIII's dialog takes 401.
      \end{panel}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.49\textwidth}
      \begin{panel}[equal height group=j2]
        \blocktitle{Only five symbols occur}
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
          \foreach \i/\t in {0/{(1,0,0)},1/{(1,1,0)},2/{(0,0,1)},3/{(1,0,1)},4/{(1,1,1)}} \node[bxa,minimum width=11mm,minimum height=5mm] at (\i*12.4,0) {$\t$};
        \end{tikzpicture}\par
        \vskip1mm\raggedright\small
        $s_2=0\Rightarrow b=1$ and $b=0\Rightarrow s=0$: five of the eight 3-bit values.
      \end{panel}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 2 detail: the same decisions build the quotient}
  \centering
  $\alpha p+\beta d_x=1\ \Longrightarrow\ \beta\equiv d_x^{-1}\pmod p\ \Longrightarrow\ \lambda=d_y\,\beta\equiv d_y/d_x\pmod p$\par
  \vskip3mm
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
    \node[bxo,minimum width=22mm,minimum height=7mm] (a) at (0,0) {walk $(u,v)$};
    \node[bxo,minimum width=22mm,minimum height=7mm] (b) at (40,0) {$(u',v')$};
    \draw[flow] (a) -- node[above,font=\scriptsize] {$M$} (b);
    \node[font=\large] at (60,0) {$\Longleftrightarrow$};
    \node[bxa,minimum width=22mm,minimum height=7mm] (c) at (80,0) {pair $(Y,Z)$};
    \node[bxa,minimum width=22mm,minimum height=7mm] (d) at (120,0) {$(Y',Z')$};
    \draw[flow] (c) -- node[above,font=\scriptsize] {$M^{-T}$} (d);
  \end{tikzpicture}\par
  \vskip3mm
  \raggedright
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \small \textbf{Linear updates.} Each halving, swap and subtraction is a linear map $M$ of $(u,v)$. The same live controls apply $M^{-T}$ to $(Y,Z)$, so as the walk reaches $(1,0)$ the pair turns $d_y$ into $\lambda$.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \small \textbf{Coherent decisions.} They depend on the quantum $d_x$, so they cannot be measured or discarded. The submission stores 261 symbols, uses them live, and replays them backwards to restore $X=d_x$.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip2mm
  {\small Built in this repo from general cells (\texttt{ec\_gcd.py}): Jump-2 takes 261 rounds, and an in-place multiplication costs $1\,161\,099$ Toffolis.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 2 detail: a base-5 transcript}
  \begin{columns}[T,onlytextwidth]
    \begin{column}{0.32\textwidth}
      \begin{panel}[equal height group=b5]
        \centering\blocktitle{1. Reachable support}
        \small A symbol has three bits but only five values:\par\vskip1mm
        \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
          \foreach \i/\t in {0/100,1/110,2/001,3/101,4/111} \node[bxa,minimum width=7.2mm,minimum height=4.5mm,font=\tiny] at (\i*8,0) {\t};
        \end{tikzpicture}\par\vskip1mm
        $\phi:\{\sigma\}\to\{0,\dots,4\}$\par\vskip1mm
        \raggedright a reversible relabelling: nothing is measured or discarded.
      \end{panel}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.32\textwidth}
      \begin{panel}[equal height group=b5]
        \centering\blocktitle{2. Pack three symbols}
        \small $d_j=\phi(\sigma_j)$, three base-5 digits:
        \[ z=d_0+5d_1+25d_2 \]
        \[ z\le124<128=2^7 \]
        $3\times3=9$ qubits $\to$ \textbf{7}.
      \end{panel}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.32\textwidth}
      \begin{panel}[equal height group=b5]
        \centering\blocktitle{3. Tape layout}
        \small For 261 symbols:
        \[ 2+86\cdot7+5=\mathbf{609} \]
        {\scriptsize step 0, 86 triples, last pair}\par\vskip1mm
        qubits, against $261\cdot3=783$ stored raw.
      \end{panel}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip2mm
  {\small Act VIII packs [1128]'s dialog the same way: three pairs of bits take 27 values, so they fit in 5 qubits.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 2 detail: streamed replay}
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm,every node/.append style={text width=19mm}]
    \node[bxo,minimum height=11mm] (fw) at (11,9) {forward walk emits $\sigma_0,\sigma_1,\dots$};
    \node[bxo,minimum height=11mm,text width=24mm] (use) at (55,19) {use each symbol at once for the $M^{-T}$ update};
    \node[bxk,minimum height=11mm] (acc) at (88,19) {accumulate the slope $Y\to\lambda$};
    \node[bxl,minimum height=11mm,text width=24mm] (enc) at (55,0) {encode each full group, release two wires};
    \node[bxk,minimum height=11mm,text width=17mm] (tau) at (82,0) {$\tau$: compressed\\transcript};
    \node[bxl,minimum height=11mm,text width=19mm] (dec) at (106,0) {decode one group when replay needs it};
    \node[bxk,minimum height=11mm,text width=17mm] (rw) at (131,0) {reverse walk, restore $X=d_x$};
    \draw[flow] (fw.east) -- (25,9) |- (use.west); \draw[flow] (fw.east) -- (25,9) |- (enc.west);
    \node[note,anchor=south,text width=14mm] at (33.5,19.3) {live controls}; \node[note,anchor=north,text width=14mm] at (33.5,-0.3) {same controls};
    \draw[flow] (use) -- (acc); \draw[flow] (enc) -- (tau); \draw[flow] (tau) -- (dec); \draw[flow] (dec) -- (rw);
\end{tikzpicture}\par
  \vskip4mm
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
    \draw[draw=body!70,fill=arfill] (0,0) rectangle (44,6); \node[font=\scriptsize] at (22,3) {shrinking $u,v$};
    \draw[draw=body!70,fill=lkfill] (44,0) rectangle (60,6); \node[font=\scriptsize] at (52,3) {growing $\tau$};
    \draw[flow] (63,3) -- (71,3);
    \draw[draw=body!70,fill=arfill] (74,0) rectangle (97,6); \node[font=\scriptsize] at (85.5,3) {remaining $u,v$};
    \draw[draw=body!70,fill=lkfill] (97,0) rectangle (136,6); \node[font=\scriptsize] at (116.5,3) {compressed tape $\tau$};
  \end{tikzpicture}\par
  \vskip2mm
  \raggedright{\small $\tau$ is quantum, entangled with $d_x$, not a classical log. As the Euclidean registers narrow, the tape grows into the space they release; the reverse replay decodes only the current group and returns the tape to $|0\rangle$.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 2 detail: width scheduling and qubit reuse}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.31\textwidth}
      \begin{panel}[equal height group=ws]
        \blocktitle{Scheduled widths}
        \small $w_i=\texttt{SCHED\_J2}[i]$: only the low $w_i$ bits of $u,v$ stay live. Lanes known to be zero are released.
      \end{panel}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.31\textwidth}
      \begin{panel}[equal height group=ws]
        \blocktitle{Narrow comparisons}
        \small $k_i<w_i$: the swap decision reads only the top $k_i$ bits. Windows calibrated on the benchmark.
      \end{panel}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.31\textwidth}
      \begin{panel}[equal height group=ws]
        \blocktitle{Hosting, cleanup}
        \small Known bits $u_0=1$, $v_0=0$ are parked; live registers lend dirty space; ancillas cleared by measurement.
      \end{panel}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip3mm
  \centering
  $Q_i\ \approx\ \underbrace{512}_{\text{Bézout pair}}\ +\ \underbrace{|\tau_i|}_{\text{compressed dialog}}\ +\ \underbrace{2w_i}_{u_i,\,v_i}\ +\ \underbrace{q_{\mathrm{work},i}}_{\text{comparison, carry, control}}$\par
  \vskip2mm
  \raggedright{\small The schedule makes the fall of $2w_i$ absorb the growth of $|\tau_i|$: [1128]'s register sharing (Act XII), pushed further.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 3: prepare the $X$ workspace}
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
    \draw[color=subtle,dashed] (16,26) -- (142,26); \node[lab,font=\small,color=subtle] at (15.5,26) {$x_A$};
    \draw[wire] (16,10) -- (142,10); \node[lab,font=\small] at (15.5,10) {$X:|d_x\rangle$};
    \draw[twire] (16,0) -- (142,0); \node[lab,font=\small,color=cverm] at (15.5,0) {$Y:|\lambda\rangle$};
    \node[bxl,minimum width=26mm,minimum height=11mm] (cl) at (34,26) {computed classically\\$3x_A\bmod p$};
    \node[bxl,minimum width=22mm,minimum height=8mm] (mat) at (64,26) {materialise\\$|3x_A\rangle_C$};
    \node[bxl,minimum width=22mm,minimum height=8mm] (un) at (126,26) {unload, free $C$\\$|3x_A\rangle_C\mapsto|0\rangle$};
    \draw[flow] (cl) -- (mat); \draw[flow] (mat) -- (un);
    \node[bxa,minimum width=36mm,minimum height=9mm] (add) at (95,10) {in-place modular addition\\$|d_x\rangle_X|3x_A\rangle_C\mapsto|d_x+3x_A\rangle_X|3x_A\rangle_C$};
    \draw[thick,color=body] (mat.south) |- (80,18) -- (80,14.5);
    \node[bx,fill=white,minimum width=26mm,minimum height=6mm] at (95,0) {$Y$ passes through};
    \node[note,anchor=south east] at (142,10.4) {$X=|x_R+2x_A\rangle$};
  \end{tikzpicture}\par
  \vskip3mm
  \begin{panel}
    \small\textbf{Why this step.} \ $d_x+3x_A=(x_R-x_A)+3x_A=x_R+2x_A$, so that the next stage, subtracting $\lambda^2$, leaves $x_R+2x_A-\lambda^2=x_A-x_{R'}$. The multiplication by 3 is done on a laptop; no quantum register holds it outside this stage. In full Shor, $3x_A$ is one more table --- the lookup Act XII puts on SELECT-SWAP.
  \end{panel}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 4: subtract the square, one Karatsuba piece at a time}
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
    \draw[twire] (16,25) -- (142,25); \node[lab,font=\small,color=cverm] at (15.5,25) {$Y:|\lambda\rangle$};
    \draw[wire] (16,0) -- (142,0); \node[lab,font=\small] at (15.5,0) {$X$};
    \node[bxl,minimum width=26mm,minimum height=9mm] (sp) at (32,25) {logical split\\$\lambda=L+2^{128}H$};
    \foreach \x/\b/\a in {58/{$S=L+H$\\build $W=S^2$}/{$X\leftarrow X-\beta W$},92/{build $U=L^2$}/{$X\leftarrow X-U+\beta U$},126/{build $V=H^2$}/{$X\leftarrow X+\beta V-FV$}} {
      \node[bxa,minimum width=26mm,minimum height=8.5mm] (b\x) at (\x,12) {\b};
      \node[bxl,minimum width=28mm,minimum height=7mm] (a\x) at (\x,0) {\a};
      \draw[flow] (sp.south) -- (b\x.north); \draw[flow] (b\x) -- (a\x);}
    \node[note,anchor=south east] at (142,25.4) {$Y$ unchanged};
\end{tikzpicture}\par
  \vskip1mm
  {\small $\lambda^2=U+\beta(W-U-V)+\beta^2V\ \equiv\ U+\beta(W-U-V)+FV\pmod p$}\par
  {\scriptsize with $\beta=2^{128}$ and $\beta^2=2^{256}\equiv F\pmod p$}\par
  \vskip1mm
  \raggedright{\small The three signed updates add up to $-\lambda^2$, taking $X$ from $x_R+2x_A$ to $x_A-x_{R'}$. Each square is built, applied and unbuilt before the next.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 4 detail: build, apply, unbuild --- and four specialisations}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm,every node/.append style={text width=60mm}]
        \foreach \i/\c/\t in {0/bxa/{\textbf{1.} Split $\lambda=L+2^{128}H$; build $S=L+H$ (129 bits)},1/bxo/{\textbf{2.} Build $W=S^2$, apply $-\beta W$, unbuild},2/bxa/{\textbf{3.} Build $U=L^2$, apply $-U+\beta U$, unbuild},3/bxo/{\textbf{4.} Build $V=H^2$, apply $\beta V-FV$, unbuild},4/bxa/{\textbf{5.} Undo $S=L+H$: all work back to $|0\rangle$},5/bxk/{\textbf{Result:} $X_{\mathrm{out}}=X_{\mathrm{in}}-\lambda^2$, \ $Y=\lambda$ kept}} {
          \node[style=\c,align=left,minimum height=6.6mm] at (0,-\i*8.2) {\t};}
      \end{tikzpicture}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.49\textwidth}
      \footnotesize
      \begin{panel}[colback=arfill]\textbf{Symmetric squaring.} $24\,512$ cross products for the three squares: $24.9\%$ fewer than one 256-bit square.\end{panel}
      \begin{panel}[colback=okfill]\textbf{Streamed.} One of $U,V,W$ live at a time; no 512-bit square is ever held.\end{panel}
      \begin{panel}[colback=lkfill]\textbf{Pseudo-Mersenne fold.} $2^{256}V$ becomes $FV$: a constant fold, not a full reduction.\end{panel}
      \begin{panel}[colback=opfill]\textbf{Sparse constant.} $F=2^{32}+2^{10}-2^6+2^4+1$: $FV$ is five shifted additions.\end{panel}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip1mm
  {\small This repo uses IonQ's squarer and fold instead ($79\,160$ Toffolis); a Karatsuba split from its general cells cost more.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 5: multiply by running a fresh division backwards}
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
    \draw[wire] (16,26) -- (142,26); \node[lab,font=\small] at (15.5,26) {$X$};
    \draw[twire] (16,11) -- (142,11); \node[lab,font=\small,color=cverm] at (15.5,11) {$Y:|\lambda\rangle$};
    \node[note,anchor=south] at (98,26.4) {$x_A-x_{R'}$};
    \draw[wire] (52,0) -- (142,0); \node[lab,font=\small] at (51.5,0) {$Z:|0\rangle$};
    \node[bxl,minimum width=18mm,minimum height=5mm] (ini) at (32,37) {initialise $u\leftarrow p$};
    \node[bxo,minimum width=27mm,minimum height=11mm] (walk) at (32,26) {forward Jump-2 walk\\on $(p,X)$\\records a fresh $\tau$};
    \draw[thick,color=body] (ini) -- (walk);
    \node[bxl,minimum width=24mm,minimum height=9mm] (tau) at (62,18) {$\tau$: dialog of\\$X=x_A-x_{R'}$};
    \node[bxk,minimum width=27mm,minimum height=11mm] (rev) at (126,26) {reverse walk\\consume $\tau$, restore $X$};
    \draw[flow] (walk.south east) -- (tau.west); \draw[flow] (tau.east) -- (rev.south west);
    \node[bxo,minimum width=28mm,minimum height=15mm] (mp) at (72,5.5) {multiplicand pair\\(after the walk)\\$(Y,Z):(\lambda,0)\mapsto(0,\lambda)$};
    \node[bxa,minimum width=30mm,minimum height=15mm] (ft) at (108,5.5) {decode $\tau$ in reverse,\\forward-transpose steps\\$|0\rangle_Y|\lambda\rangle_Z\mapsto|\lambda X\rangle_Y|0\rangle_Z$};
    \draw[flow] (tau.south east) -- (ft.north west);
    \node[note,anchor=south east] at (142,11.4) {$Y=|\lambda(x_A-x_{R'})\rangle$};
  \end{tikzpicture}\par
  \vskip2mm
  $U_{\mathrm{div}}:\ |X\rangle|Y\rangle\mapsto|X\rangle|Y X^{-1}\bmod p\rangle \qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad U_{\mathrm{div}}^{-1}:\ |X\rangle|Y\rangle\mapsto|X\rangle|Y X\bmod p\rangle$\par
  \vskip1mm
  \raggedright{\small Reversibility turns the low-space division of stage 2 into a multiplication. $X$ is a different operand now, so a fresh transcript $\tau(X)$ is built and replayed.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 5 detail: what running division backwards buys}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \begin{panel}[equal height group=s5]
        \blocktitle{It saves width}
        \small The in-place map reuses $X$, $Y$, $Z$ and $\tau$. A straightforward out-of-place multiplication would need an extra 256-qubit product register and its cleanup. Peak width falls.
      \end{panel}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \begin{panel}[equal height group=s5]
        \blocktitle{It does not save Toffolis}
        \small Building and replaying the transcript remains a dominant gate cost. Cleanup is exact; the product relation needs the 261-step walk to converge, which is validated on the benchmark's inputs.
      \end{panel}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip3mm
  \begin{takeaway}The same trade as Act VIII's in-place multiplication: dialog forwards, replay, dialog backwards --- one circuit gives both multiplicative steps of a point addition.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Stage 6: recover the affine output}
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]
    \draw[twire] (16,24) -- (142,24); \node[lab,font=\small,color=cverm] at (15.5,24) {$Y$};
    \draw[wire] (16,0) -- (142,0); \node[lab,font=\small] at (15.5,0) {$X$};
    \node[note,anchor=south west] at (16,24.4) {$\lambda(x_A-x_{R'})$}; \node[note,anchor=south west] at (16,0.4) {$x_A-x_{R'}$};
    \node[bxl,minimum width=18mm,minimum height=7mm] (ly) at (40,34) {load $|y_A\rangle_C$};
    \node[bxa,minimum width=28mm,minimum height=8mm] (sy) at (70,24) {modular subtraction\\$Y\leftarrow Y-y_A=y_{R'}$};
    \node[bxl,minimum width=18mm,minimum height=7mm] (uy) at (104,34) {unload, free};
    \draw[thick,color=body] (ly.south) |- (60,30) -- (60,28); \draw[flow] (ly) -- (uy);
    \node[bxl,minimum width=34mm,minimum height=9mm] (cc) at (44,11) {classically $c=x_A+1\bmod 2^{256}$,\\load $|c\rangle_C$};
    \node[bxa,minimum width=24mm,minimum height=8mm] (cp) at (44,0) {complement\\$X\leftarrow2^{256}-1-X$};
    \node[bxa,minimum width=26mm,minimum height=8mm] (va) at (82,0) {vented add with carry\\$X\leftarrow\overline X+c$};
    \node[bxk,minimum width=30mm,minimum height=8mm] (pm) at (120,0) {pseudo-Mersenne fold\\$X\leftarrow x_A-X\pmod p$};
    \draw[thick,color=body] (cc.east) -| (82,4);
    \node[note,anchor=south east] at (142,24.4) {$Y=|y_{R'}\rangle$};
  \end{tikzpicture}\par
  \vskip1.5mm
  $|x_A-x_{R'}\rangle_X\,|\lambda(x_A-x_{R'})\rangle_Y\ \longmapsto\ |x_{R'}\rangle_X\,|y_{R'}\rangle_Y$\par
  \vskip1mm
  \raggedright{\small \textbf{Fused reverse subtraction:} $\overline X+(x_A+1)=x_A-X \pmod{2^{256}}$, one complement and one addition, then the secp256k1 correction. Every loaded coordinate, carry and reduction flag is gone before the two outputs are returned.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{The 1\,151-qubit entry, read against this repo}
  \centering\footnotesize
  \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
  \begin{tabular}{@{}lllr@{}}
    \toprule
    circuit & what is counted & qubits & Toffolis executed \\
    \midrule
    ECDSA.fail \texttt{8e9c9a2} & addition, classical addend & $1\,151$ & $1\,299\,453$ (average) \\
    this repo, IonQ cells & windowed addition with lookups & $1\,457$ & $1\,310\,693$ \\
    \quad of which lookups and repair & & & $197\,364$ \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}\par
  \vskip3mm
  \raggedright
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \small \textbf{Not the same object.} The entry leaves out the coherent lookup of $A$; with one, each classical load becomes a table read, and $3x_A$ and $x_A+1$ become table entries.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \small \textbf{Not the same evidence.} The entry's 261-step walk, comparison windows and 19-bit tests are validated on the benchmark's inputs; this repo's cells are checked on every input at toy primes.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \begin{takeaway}What carries over intact: the Jump-2 walk, the base-5 tape, streamed replay, the Karatsuba square and carry venting.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Five blueprints, one table}
  \centering\small
  \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
  \begin{tabular}{@{}llllrr@{}}
    \toprule
    blueprint & target & code, hardware & cycle & phys.\ qubits & runtime \\
    \midrule
    Gidney--Ekerå 2019 & RSA & surface, supercond. & $1\,\mu$s & $2.0\times10^7$ & 7.4 h \\
    Gidney 2025 & RSA & surface$+$yoked, supercond. & $1\,\mu$s & $897\,864$ & 4.96 d \\
    Pinnacle 2026 & RSA & qLDPC, supercond.-like & $1\,\mu$s & $\approx94\,000$ & $\approx$1 month \\
    Google 2026 & ECC & surface$+$yoked, supercond. & $1\,\mu$s & $<500\,000$ & 18--23 min \\
    Oratomic 2026 & ECC & qLDPC, neutral atoms & 1 ms & $\approx26\,000$ & few days$^\ast$ \\
    Oratomic 2026 & RSA & qLDPC, neutral atoms & 1 ms & $\approx102\,000$ & 97 d$^\ast$ \\
    IonQ 2026 & ECC & qLDPC, trapped ions & 6.7 ms & $19\,397$ & 25.7 d$^\dagger$ \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
  \vskip2mm
  \raggedright{\footnotesize Superconducting rows: $p=10^{-3}$, $10\,\mu$s reaction. $^\ast$Many Toffolis in parallel; serially the 12\,000-atom version takes most of a year. $^\dagger$Per attempt (41--63\% success), at $p=10^{-4}$.}
  \begin{takeaway}Same logical circuits, $10^4$ to $10^6$ physical qubits.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{What sets the time per Toffoli}
  \[
    t_{\mathrm{run}}\approx\frac{T_{\mathrm{Toff}}\;t_{\mathrm{Toff}}}{P},\qquad
    t_{\mathrm{Toff}}\gtrsim\max\{\underbrace{(1+\eta)\,t_{\mathrm{react}}}_{\text{reaction time}},\ \underbrace{1/R_{\mathrm{magic}}}_{\text{factory output}},\ \underbrace{t_{\mathrm{surgery}}}_{\text{one logical op}}\}
  \]
  \centering\small
  \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
  \begin{tabular}{@{}lrrrl@{}}
    \toprule
    blueprint & Toffolis & runtime & $t_{\mathrm{Toff}}$ & bound by \\
    \midrule
    Google 2026 (ECC) & $7\times10^7$ & 18 min & $15\,\mu$s & reaction time ($1.5\times10\,\mu$s) \\
    Gidney 2025 (RSA) & $6.5\times10^9$ & 4.63 d & $62\,\mu$s & few factories, to save qubits \\
    IonQ 2026 (ECC) & $3.9\times10^7$ & 25.7 d & 29.5 ms & logical measurements, transport \\
    Oratomic serial (ECC) & $8\times10^7$ & $\sim$230 d & 0.25 s & $\approx250$ surgery cycles at 1 ms \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
  \begin{takeaway}$P$ Toffolis run in parallel; $R_{\mathrm{magic}}$ magic states per second. Fast clock: one Toffoli per reaction time. Few factories: factory-bound. Slow clock: surgery-bound --- only parallelism helps.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Physical qubits against time}
  \begin{columns}[c]
    \begin{column}{0.6\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}
      \begin{axis}[scale only axis,width=0.82\linewidth,height=3.6cm,xmode=log,ymode=log,xmin=5e3,xmax=3e9,ymin=5e-3,ymax=3e4,
        xlabel={physical qubits},ylabel={runtime (days)},grid=both,clip mode=individual,
        legend style={at={(0.5,-0.27)},anchor=north,legend columns=3,/tikz/every even column/.append style={column sep=4pt}}]
        \addlegendimage{only marks,mark=square*,mark size=2.4pt,subtle}\addlegendentry{surface code}
        \addlegendimage{only marks,mark=diamond*,mark size=3pt,subtle}\addlegendentry{Pinnacle}
        \addlegendimage{only marks,mark=triangle*,mark size=3pt,subtle}\addlegendentry{Oratomic}
        \addlegendimage{only marks,mark=pentagon*,mark size=2.8pt,subtle}\addlegendentry{cat qubits}
        \addlegendimage{only marks,mark=star,mark size=3.6pt,thick,subtle}\addlegendentry{IonQ}
        \addplot[thick,dashed,cverm!60,forget plot] coordinates {(13255,3e4) (13255,1e4) (13436,1e4) (13436,177) (94000,177) (94000,30) (897864,30) (897864,4.96) (2e7,4.96) (2e7,0.31) (3e9,0.31)};
        \addplot[thick,cblue!60,forget plot] coordinates {(11961,3e4) (11961,230) (19397,230) (19397,25.7) (26000,25.7) (26000,3) (126133,3) (126133,0.375) (5e5,0.375) (5e5,0.016) (3e9,0.016)};
        \addplot[only marks,mark=square*,mark size=2.4pt,cverm,forget plot] coordinates {(1e9,1.1) (2.3e8,3.7) (1.7e8,1) (2e7,0.31) (897864,4.96)};
        \addplot[only marks,mark=diamond*,mark size=3pt,cverm,forget plot] coordinates {(94000,30)};
        \addplot[only marks,mark=triangle*,mark size=3pt,cverm,forget plot] coordinates {(102000,97) (13255,1e4)};
        \addplot[only marks,mark=pentagon*,mark size=2.8pt,cverm,forget plot] coordinates {(13436,177)};
        \addplot[only marks,mark=square*,mark size=2.4pt,cblue,forget plot] coordinates {(5e5,0.016)};
        \addplot[only marks,mark=pentagon*,mark size=2.8pt,cblue,forget plot] coordinates {(126133,0.375)};
        \addplot[only marks,mark=triangle*,mark size=3pt,cblue,forget plot] coordinates {(26000,3) (11961,230)};
        \addplot[only marks,mark=star,mark size=3.6pt,thick,cblue,forget plot] coordinates {(19397,25.7)};
        \node[font=\scriptsize,anchor=south east] at (axis cs:2.9e9,1.6) {Fowler 2012};
        \node[font=\scriptsize,anchor=north] at (axis cs:2e7,0.24) {GE 2019};
        \node[font=\scriptsize,anchor=south east] at (axis cs:880000,5.3) {Gidney 2025};
        \node[font=\scriptsize,anchor=west] at (axis cs:6e5,0.016) {Google};
      \end{axis}
      \end{tikzpicture}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.36\textwidth}
      \small
      \textcolor{cverm}{Red}: RSA-2048; \textcolor{cblue}{blue}: ECDLP-256. Staircases are the frontiers; they mix clocks $10^3$ apart and error rates $10\times$ apart.
      \vskip2mm
      In qubits $\times$ cycles, Pinnacle and serial Oratomic are within $2$--$3\times$ of the surface-code baselines; the $\sim100\times$ cheaper points assume many logical operations at once.
      \vskip2mm
      {\footnotesize Cat qubits: superconducting qubits biased against bit flips.}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \resolved{Least tested: long-range connectivity, real-time decoding, parallel surgery.}
\end{frame}

% =====================================================================================
\act{Act XII --- the 2026 optimisations, built and measured in this repo}

\begin{frame}{Act XII --- from the papers as printed to below IonQ's count}
  \begin{difficulty}{12}Act VIII built eprints 2026/106 and 2026/1128 as printed: $2.6$M Toffolis per point addition. IonQ reports $1.39$M on $1\,457$ qubits. Where is the factor 2?\end{difficulty}
  \centering\small
  \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.05}
  \begin{tabular}{@{}lrr@{}}
    \toprule
    one windowed point addition, $n=256$, $w=16$ & before & after \\
    \midrule
    two in-place multiplications & 3\,910\,552 & 1\,441\,130 \\
    square-subtract & 1\,050\,623 & 79\,160 \\
    table lookups & 1\,310\,700 & 197\,364 \\
    five modular additions & 5\,115 & 5\,115 \\
    \midrule
    total & 6\,276\,990 & \textbf{1\,722\,769} \\
    whole ECDLP-256 ($34\to28$ additions) & 213M & \textbf{48M} \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
  \begin{takeaway}$3.6\times$ per addition from three changes: measured uncomputation, a cheaper GCD, a squarer.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{How every optimisation is checked}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.42\textwidth}
      \blocktitle{A switch, not a fork}
      Each optimisation is a switch beside the construction it refines. Switch it off and the circuit is the old one, gate for gate.
      \vskip3mm
      \blocktitle{Exhaustively, at toy primes}
      Every basis input, every ancilla back to $|0\rangle$; a regression test pins every gate count quoted here.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.54\textwidth}
      \begin{greyblock}
        \blocktitle{Measurements leave phases}
        The exhaustive simulator cannot see phases, so each gate carries a unitary definition for the tests and a measurement-based \emph{price}. A sparse state-vector simulator runs the real measure-and-repair and checks that every basis input returns with the same phase. An approximate repair fails exactly on the inputs the tests predict.
      \end{greyblock}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{1. Lookups: ten recomputed $\to$ three loads and one repair}
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1.45mm,y=1.3mm,font=\footnotesize]
    \node[anchor=west,color=subtle] at (0,15) {before: each lookup undone by running it again --- $10L$ Toffolis, $L=2^{16}$};
    \foreach \i in {0,...,9}{\draw[fill=cverm!15,draw=cverm] (\i*6.5,8) rectangle (\i*6.5+5,12);
      \node[font=\scriptsize] at (\i*6.5+2.5,10) {$L$};}
    \node[anchor=west,color=subtle] at (0,3.5) {after: load, use, measure in $X$; one merged phase repair --- $3L+2\sqrt L$ Toffolis};
    \foreach \i in {0,1,2}{\draw[fill=cverm!15,draw=cverm] (\i*14,-4) rectangle (5+\i*14,0);
      \node[font=\scriptsize] at (2.5+\i*14,-2) {$L$};
      \node[meter,minimum size=5mm,font=\scriptsize] at (9.5+\i*14,-2) {$X$};}
    \draw[fill=cpurple!12,draw=cpurple] (43,-4) rectangle (62,0); \node at (52.5,-2) {phase fix, $2\sqrt L$};
  \end{tikzpicture}
  \vskip3mm
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.58\textwidth}
      \raggedright $X$-measuring a loaded register costs nothing but leaves the phase $(-1)^{m\cdot T[a]}$ on the address. A \emph{phase lookup} of $F[a]=m\cdot T[a]$ repairs it in $\approx2\sqrt L$ ANDs, against $L$ for a lookup. IonQ's step: the three unloads share one address, so their tables are XORed and one repair serves all three.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.38\textwidth}
      \begin{peachblock}\peachtitle{Measured, $n=256$, $w=16$}
        $1\,310\,700\to\mathbf{197\,364}$ Toffolis per addition.
      \end{peachblock}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}[fragile]{2. The GCD: conditionally inverted steps}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.53\textwidth}
      Two thirds of a point addition is the in-place multiplier, most of it the walk and replay of a binary GCD.
      \vskip2mm
      IonQ store $\tilde v=v+(1-a)u$, $a=v\bmod2$: both registers stay odd, the next parity is two CNOTs, and each update $\tilde v\leftarrow(\tilde v\pm u)/2$ is one \emph{uncontrolled} adder between two rows of CNOTs.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.43\textwidth}
      \code[basicstyle=\ttfamily\footnotesize]{ci_add.py}
      \vskip1mm
      {\small $n-1$ ANDs, where [1128] spends $2n$ on a controlled subtraction. The recorded bits are exactly the dialog's, so its replay and packing carry over.}
      \begin{peachblock}\peachtitle{Measured, $n=256$}
        in-place multiplication: $1\,955\,276\to\mathbf{720\,565}$
      \end{peachblock}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Five GCDs behind one interface}
  \centering\small
  \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
  \begin{tabular}{@{}lrr@{}}
    \toprule
    in-place multiplication, $n=256$ & rounds & Toffolis \\
    \midrule
    dialog [1128], exact, full width & 401 & 1\,955\,276 \\
    $+$ fused 77-bit compares, width schedule, pseudo-Mersenne & 401 & 865\,588 \\
    conditionally inverted (IonQ), IonQ replay & 401 & \textbf{720\,565} \\
    ping-pong (ECDSA.Fail) & 704 & 917\,565 \\
    Jump-2 (ECDSA.Fail) & 261 & 1\,161\,099 \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
  \vskip3mm
  \raggedright \texttt{ec\_gcd.py} holds [1128]'s dialog, IonQ's conditionally inverted walk, and ECDSA.Fail's ping-pong and Jump-2 (with its base-5 transcript codec).
  \begin{takeaway}Ping-pong is cheapest per round but needs $2.75n$ rounds --- and exhaustively, $n=16$ needs $3.9n$.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{The GCD, as a circuit: walk, record, replay}
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=0.93mm,y=1.0mm,font=\footnotesize]
    \tikzset{reg/.style={draw=body,fill=greybox,minimum height=6mm,anchor=west,inner sep=2pt}}
    \node[reg,minimum width=27mm] (u) at (0,16) {$u$ (the prime $q$)};
    \node[reg,minimum width=27mm] (v) at (0,9) {$\tilde v$ (the input $x$)};
    \node[reg,minimum width=27mm,fill=cpurple!10,draw=cpurple] (r) at (0,2) {record $a_i,m_i$};
    \foreach \i in {0,...,5}{
      \pgfmathsetmacro{\x}{33+\i*8}
      \draw[fill=cverm!12,draw=cverm] (\x,6.5) rectangle (\x+6.5,19); \node[rotate=90,font=\scriptsize] at (\x+3.25,12.75) {ci add};
      \draw[cpurple,-{Stealth[length=1.2mm]}] (\x+3.25,6.5) -- (\x+3.25,4.6);
      \draw[fill=cpurple!25,draw=cpurple] (\x+1.5,1.5) rectangle (\x+5,4.5);}
    \node[color=subtle,anchor=west] at (0,23) {walk: 401 rounds, $u,\tilde v$ narrow};
    \draw[-{Stealth[length=1.8mm]},thick,color=body] (83,12.75) -- (90,12.75);
    \node[reg,minimum width=24mm] (s) at (92,16) {$r,s$ (Bézout)};
    \node[reg,minimum width=24mm] (y) at (92,9) {$y$};
    \foreach \i in {0,...,3}{
      \pgfmathsetmacro{\x}{119+\i*8}
      \draw[fill=cblue!12,draw=cblue] (\x,6.5) rectangle (\x+6.5,19); \node[rotate=90,font=\scriptsize] at (\x+3.25,12.75) {$\pm$, $/2$};
      \draw[cpurple,-{Stealth[length=1.2mm]}] (\x+3.25,4.6) -- (\x+3.25,6.5);
      \draw[fill=cpurple!25,draw=cpurple] (\x+1.5,1.5) rectangle (\x+5,4.5);}
    \node[color=subtle,anchor=west] at (92,23) {replay: 401 signed steps on $y$};
    \node[color=subtle] at (75,-3.5) {then the walk runs backwards to clear $u,\tilde v$, and a second walk unrecords: $x\cdot y\bmod q$ in place};
  \end{tikzpicture}
  \vskip3mm
  \raggedright\small
  \begin{itemize}
    \item The record is packed 5 Toffolis a round ([1128] Fig.~1) and grows into the qubits the narrowing walk frees: \emph{register sharing}.
    \item IonQ's replay cells: a phase-approximate adder whose carry is the reduction flag, 352 Toffolis a step against 712.
    \item It cannot see $x+y=p$, met once in the first 37 steps: those keep an exact ``careful'' cell.
  \end{itemize}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{3. Squaring: a dedicated squarer and a pseudo-Mersenne fold}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.52\textwidth}
      Step 10 subtracts $\lambda^2$; with the general multiplier that is two multiplications. IonQ's squarer:
      \[ x^2=-x+\sum_i(2x_i-1)\,2^{2i+1}(h_i+x_i) \]
      with $h_i=\lfloor x/2^{i+1}\rfloor$. Every term is an \emph{unconditional} signed subtraction on a window of $n+1-i$ bits: $\tfrac12n(n+3)$ ANDs. A fold $2^{256}\equiv2^{32}+977$ reduces into the accumulator.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.44\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1.3mm,y=1.3mm,font=\scriptsize]
        \draw[fill=greybox,draw=body] (0,-1.2) rectangle (40,1.2); \node[color=subtle] at (20,-3.5) {$z$, $2n+1$ bits};
        \foreach \i in {0,...,5}{\draw[fill=cverm!15,draw=cverm] (\i*4,3+\i*2.6) rectangle (40-\i*2,5.2+\i*2.6);}
        \node[color=subtle,align=center] at (20,21) {signed windows of $n+1-i$ bits,\\shifted by $2i+1$, subtracted};
      \end{tikzpicture}
      \begin{peachblock}\peachtitle{Measured, $n=256$}
        square-subtract: $1\,050\,623\to\mathbf{79\,160}$ Toffolis.
      \end{peachblock}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}[fragile]{The squarer, in code}
  \code[basicstyle=\ttfamily\footnotesize]{sqr_int.py}
  \vskip2mm
  Row $i$: flip $h$ by $x_i$ so the operand is $\pm(h_i+x_i)$ with sign $x_i$, subtract it from the window of $z$ at offset $2i+1$, flip back.
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}[fragile]{Building 256 bits on a laptop: one gate per shape}
  \small One 256-bit point addition is tens of millions of gates but few distinct pieces: the replay calls one modular adder 800 times. \texttt{hier.tracing()} wraps the existing builders; each call is built once per \emph{shape} and appended as one opaque gate.\par
  \vskip1mm
  \code[basicstyle=\ttfamily\fontsize{7.6}{8.8}\selectfont]{boundary.py}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      {\small \textbf{Shape}: register widths, shared qubits, classical parameters. Run backwards, the gate is inverted.}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      {\small \textbf{Checked}: equal to the flat count at toy size (8 configurations); Qualtran agrees on the exported bloqs.}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{The first full-size builds}
  \centering\footnotesize
  \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.12}
  \begin{tabular}{@{}lrr@{}}
    \toprule
    one windowed point addition, secp256k1, $w=16$ & Toffolis & qubits \\
    \midrule
    Qualtran's \texttt{ECAdd} (Litinski 2023), for reference & 8\,346\,972 & 1\,798 \\
    dialog [1128], exact arithmetic, MBU lookups & 5\,163\,617 & 2\,104 \\
    \quad $+$ 77-bit comparisons, width schedule, pseudo-Mersenne replay & 2\,972\,327 & 2\,251 \\
    \quad $+$ dedicated squarer and fold & 2\,000\,864 & 2\,251 \\
    conditionally inverted walk and replay (IonQ), squarer & \textbf{1\,711\,194} & 2\,251 \\
    ping-pong walk, 704 rounds (ECDSA.Fail), squarer & 2\,117\,534 & 2\,155 \\
    dialog, register sharing, Fig.~1 packing, squarer & 2\,006\,184 & \textbf{1\,873} \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}\par
  \vskip2mm
  \raggedright\small
  The whole algorithm builds too: 28 windowed additions, $47\,978\,966$ Toffolis on $2\,251$ qubits. On the same tree Qualtran counts $249\,846\,912$ Cliffords and $29\,873\,480$ measurements.
  \begin{takeaway}Two gaps remain: the best row is $23\%$ above IonQ's count, and every row needs more qubits than any paper reports.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{4. Matching IonQ: the gap is in the replay}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.55\textwidth}
      \centering\footnotesize
      \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.12}
      \begin{tabular}{@{}lrr@{}}
        \toprule
        $n=256$, Toffolis & before & after \\
        \midrule
        lookups, repair & 197\,364 & 197\,364 \\
        GCD walks, undone & 593\,432 & 593\,432 \\
        Bézout replays & 833\,312 & \textbf{564\,086} \\
        \quad of which signed additions & 569\,600 & 307\,574 \\
        square-subtract & 79\,160 & 79\,160 \\
        everything else & 7\,926 & 4\,571 \\
        \midrule
        one point addition & 1\,711\,194 & \textbf{1\,438\,613} \\
        \bottomrule
      \end{tabular}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.41\textwidth}
      \small The walk is already IonQ's; the replay is not. Each of its 800 steps adds $\pm r$ into $s$ modulo $p$, and the cell that did it costs \textbf{712} Toffolis --- of which only 256 are the addition.
      \vskip2mm
      The rest are top-bit tests: does the sum need reducing, is it exactly $p$, was the input $0$?
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip2mm
  {\small\raggedright After: $1\,426\,965$ executed, within $2.5\%$ of IonQ's $1\,392\,608$. The 28-addition algorithm: $40\,346\,698$ Toffolis, against IonQ's $39.0\times10^6$.\par}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{IonQ's modular adder: the carry \emph{is} the flag}
  \centering
  \begin{tikzpicture}[x=10mm,y=7mm]
    \foreach \y/\l in {2/{$x$},1/{$y$},0/{$|0\rangle$}} {\node[lab] at (0,\y) {\l};}
    \draw[wire] (0,2) -- (8.6,2); \draw[wire] (0,1) -- (8.6,1); \draw[wire] (0,0) -- (4.3,0);
    \node[op,minimum width=12mm,minimum height=19mm] at (1.2,1) {ADD};
    \node[note,anchor=north] at (1.2,-0.45) {carry-out};
    \draw[thick,color=body] (3,0) -- (3,0.7); \node[dot] at (3,0) {};
    \node[op,minimum width=13mm,minimum height=6mm] at (3,1) {$+f$};
    \node[note,anchor=north] at (3,-0.45) {low $\kappa$ bits};
    \node[meter] (m) at (4.6,0) {$X$};
    \draw[clw] (m.east) -- (6.6,0) -- (6.6,0.85);
    \node[op,minimum width=19mm,minimum height=13mm] at (6.6,1.5) {$(-1)^{[y'<x]}$};
    \node[note,anchor=north] at (6.6,-0.45) {only if $m=1$; top $\delta$ bits};
  \end{tikzpicture}
  \vskip3mm
  \raggedright
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \small If $x+y\ge2^{256}$ the carry is set, and subtracting $p$ is ``drop the carry, add $f=2^{32}+977$'': only the low $\kappa=65$ bits move. No test decides the reduction.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \small After the correction the carry equals $[y'<x]$: reduced, $y'=x+y-p<x$; not, $y'\ge x$. So it is $X$-measured, and half the time a $\delta=32$-bit comparator repairs the phase.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \begin{takeaway}352 Toffolis worst case, 336 counting what executes (IonQ's convention), against 712.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}[fragile]{IonQ's modular adder, in code}
  \code[basicstyle=\ttfamily\fontsize{7.6}{8.8}\selectfont]{modadd_pm_phase.py}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      {\small Approximate only to order $f/p$ or $2^{-\delta}$: sums in $[p,2^{256})$, a carry past bit $\kappa$, a tie in the top $\delta$ bits.}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      {\small At toy primes: wrong on exactly the sums in $[p,2^n)$, phase-correct everywhere else, every measurement performed.}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{The one case that is not rare: 37 careful steps}
  \begin{columns}[c]
    \begin{column}{0.5\textwidth}
      \small The replay must pass through $0$. Dividing, the first swap moves a $0$ into $s$, and complementing it gives $2^{256}-1$, not a residue. Multiplying, the same step adds $x+y=p$ exactly: the one sum the carry cannot see.
      \vskip2mm
      The step is fixed by the 2-adic valuation of $x$: within the first $k$ steps with probability $1-2^{-k}$. So the first 37 steps keep the exact cell (IonQ: ``rounds with $x+y=p$: 37'').
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.46\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}
        \begin{axis}[scale only axis,width=0.7\linewidth,height=3.3cm,ymode=log,xmin=0,xmax=42,ymin=1e-13,ymax=2,
          xlabel={careful steps $k$},ylabel={$P(\text{not yet met})$},ytick={1,1e-4,1e-8,1e-12},
          grid=major,tick label style={font=\scriptsize},label style={font=\scriptsize}]
          \addplot[very thick,cpurple,domain=0:42,samples=43] {2^(-x)};
          \draw[cverm,thick,dashed] (axis cs:37,1e-13) -- (axis cs:37,2);
          \node[font=\scriptsize,cverm,anchor=east] at (axis cs:36,1e-4) {$2^{-37}=7\times10^{-12}$};
        \end{axis}
      \end{tikzpicture}\par
      {\scriptsize measured: the failure rate halves per careful step, zero at 37}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip2mm
  {\small Multiplying gets its \textbf{own} replay: the inverse of ``measure, sometimes repair'' is ``recompute'', which always runs. Signed additions $569\,600\to307\,574$.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Signed windows: half the table, and never $O$}
  \begin{columns}[c]
    \begin{column}{0.5\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[x=6.2mm,y=6mm]
        \node[lab,font=\scriptsize] at (-0.2,1.5) {address $i$};
        \node[lab,font=\scriptsize] at (-0.2,0) {digit $d$};
        \foreach \i/\dd in {0/-7,1/-5,2/-3,3/-1,4/1,5/3,6/5,7/7} {
          \draw[draw=body,fill=greybox] (\i,1.2) rectangle (\i+0.9,1.8); \node[font=\scriptsize] at (\i+0.45,1.5) {\i};
          \draw[thick,-{Stealth[length=1.2mm]},color=subtle] (\i+0.45,1.15) -- (\i+0.45,0.35);
          \ifnum\i<4 \draw[draw=cblue,fill=cblue!12] (\i,-0.3) rectangle (\i+0.9,0.3); \else \draw[draw=cverm,fill=cverm!12] (\i,-0.3) rectangle (\i+0.9,0.3); \fi
          \node[font=\scriptsize] at (\i+0.45,0) {$\dd$};}
        \draw[decorate,decoration={brace,amplitude=3pt,mirror},thick,color=cblue] (0.05,-0.45) -- (3.85,-0.45) node[midway,below=3pt,font=\scriptsize,color=cblue] {negate $y$};
        \draw[decorate,decoration={brace,amplitude=3pt,mirror},thick,color=cverm] (4.05,-0.45) -- (7.85,-0.45) node[midway,below=3pt,font=\scriptsize,color=cverm] {table of 4};
      \end{tikzpicture}\par
      \vskip1mm
      {\scriptsize $w=3$: $d=2i+1-2^w$, looked up by $|d|$ on $w-1$ bits}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.46\textwidth}
      \small Read the $w$ address bits as an odd digit. The lookup runs over $|d|$; the sign is a negation of $y$ before and after, and consecutive windows merge theirs into one, controlled by the XOR of the signs.
      \vskip2mm
      $2^{15}$ entries instead of $2^{16}$, and odd digits never give $O$.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip3mm
  \begin{peachblock}[fontupper=\small]\peachtitle{Measured, $n=256$}
    One addition: $1\,340\,563$ Toffolis, $1\,328\,915$ executed --- $4.6\%$ below IonQ; $2\,745\,400$ fewer over the 28 additions.
  \end{peachblock}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}[fragile]{Signed windows, in code --- and the condition they need}
  \code[basicstyle=\ttfamily\fontsize{7.6}{8.8}\selectfont]{signed.py}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      {\small Needs $y\neq0$: every visited point of odd order. secp256k1 has prime order.}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      {\small Order-43 curve, every input: one run finds $k$ $43\%$ of the time (ideal $77\%$).}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Where the 1\,457 qubits go}

  \vskip3mm
  \raggedright
  The peak moved four times, and none of the fixes adds a Toffoli:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item the careful cell's scratch is freed after its adder (259 qubits, not 647);
    \item the replay's opening and closing copies are $n$ CNOTs, not exact modular additions;
    \item the fold's 8 subtractions run on the phase adder;
    \item the walk's $u$ is shared with the record.
  \end{itemize}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{5. SELECT-SWAP lookups: trade qubits that are free anyway}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.5\textwidth}
      \small A lookup over the high $w-k$ address bits writes $2^k$ words at once; $k$ layers of Fredkins on the low bits move the addressed one into place: $2^{w-k}-2$ ANDs $+(2^k-1)b$ Fredkins.
      \vskip2mm
      The other words are a known function of the address: $X$-measured into the merged repair. The extra qubits live only during the load; at the $3x$ load only 798 are live, so $k=1$ is free under the $1\,457$ peak.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.46\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[x=1.05mm,y=1.05mm,font=\scriptsize]
        \draw[fill=cverm!15,draw=cverm] (0,10) rectangle (20,17); \node[align=center] at (10,13.5) {lookup\\on $a_{\mathrm{hi}}$};
        \foreach \j in {0,1,2,3}{\draw[fill=greybox,draw=body] (24+\j*8.5,10) rectangle (31.5+\j*8.5,17); \node[align=center] at (27.75+\j*8.5,13.5) {word\\\j};}
        \draw[-{Stealth[length=1.2mm]}] (20,13.5) -- (24,13.5);
        \draw[fill=cblue!12,draw=cblue] (24,1) rectangle (57.5,7); \node at (40.75,4) {$k$ layers of Fredkins on $a_{\mathrm{lo}}$};
        \draw[-{Stealth[length=1.2mm]}] (27.75,10) -- (27.75,7); \draw[-{Stealth[length=1.2mm]}] (53.25,10) -- (53.25,7);
      \end{tikzpicture}
      \vskip2mm
      \footnotesize
      \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.1}
      \begin{tabular}{@{}lrrr@{}}
        \toprule
        $k$ (points, $3x$) & qubits & Toffolis & executed \\
        \midrule
        (0, 0) & 1\,457 & 1\,338\,469 & 1\,326\,821 \\
        (0, 1) & \textbf{1\,457} & 1\,322\,341 & \textbf{1\,310\,693} \\
        (1, 1) & 1\,565 & 1\,290\,597 & 1\,278\,949 \\
        (2, 2) & 2\,588 & 1\,268\,581 & 1\,256\,933 \\
        (3, 3) & 4\,635 & 1\,261\,413 & 1\,249\,765 \\
        \bottomrule
      \end{tabular}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}[fragile]{SELECT-SWAP, in code}
  \code[basicstyle=\ttfamily\footnotesize]{select_swap.py}
  \vskip2mm
  Layer $i$ swaps block $j$ with block $j+2^i$ when address bit $i$ is set: after $k$ layers block 0 holds the addressed word. \texttt{unlookup} measures all blocks out and repairs the phases in one pass.
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Chasing the peak: take the scratch out of whatever sets it}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[y=9.5mm,
          pk/.style={draw=cpurple,fill=cpurple!10,thick,rounded corners=1.5pt,minimum width=25mm,minimum height=6mm,font=\small},
          why/.style={font=\scriptsize,color=subtle,anchor=west,align=left,text width=26mm}]
        \foreach \y/\t/\w in {4/replay/{record $+\,2n$\\$+$ cell scratch},3/square-subtract/{$2n$ scratch beside\\the $2n$-bit square},2/dialog walk/{copied its operand\\before subtracting},1/replay/{now the constant\\adder},0/squarer/{last}} {
          \node[pk] (n\y) at (0,\y) {\t};
          \node[why] at (1.45,\y) {\w};}
        \foreach \a/\b in {4/3,3/2,2/1,1/0} \draw[-{Stealth[length=1.6mm]},thick,color=body] (n\a) -- (n\b);
      \end{tikzpicture}\par
      {\scriptsize where the peak sat, in the order it moved}\par
      \vskip3mm
      \raggedright{\small Each new cell gives the output of the one it replaces on every input tried, failures included.}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.49\textwidth}
      \small The qubit count is a statement about \emph{one moment}; every other step can borrow.
      \vskip2mm
      \blocktitle{The cells that come out}
      \begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0.6mm}
        \item CDKM adders where no ancilla is free;
        \item a comparator on the CDKM carry chain: 1 ancilla, not $k$;
        \item an all-ones test in $\sqrt k$ chunks, ANDs parked in flags: $2\sqrt k$ ancillas, not $k-1$;
        \item a constant adder over the 33 bits of $f$, its carry added above by an \textbf{increment on borrowed qubits}.
      \end{itemize}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}[fragile]{An increment on borrowed qubits}
  \begin{columns}[c]
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[x=10mm,y=8mm]
        \node[lab] at (0,1) {$w$}; \node[lab] at (0,0) {$g$ (dirty)};
        \draw[wire] (0,1) -- (5,1); \draw[wire] (0,0) -- (5,0);
        \node[op,minimum width=10mm,minimum height=13mm] at (1,0.5) {$-g$};
        \node[hop] at (2.1,0) {$X$};
        \node[op,minimum width=10mm,minimum height=13mm] at (3.2,0.5) {$-g$};
        \node[hop] at (4.3,0) {$X$};
      \end{tikzpicture}\par
      \vskip2mm
      {\small $w-g-\overline g=w+1-2^{|w|}$\\for $g$ in \emph{any} state}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.48\textwidth}
      \small $g+\overline g$ is all ones, so two subtractions of a dirty register add 1 to $w$, and $g$ ends as it began (Gidney 2015).
      \vskip2mm
      No clean scratch: the qubits are borrowed from registers that are idle at that moment, whatever they hold.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \vskip2mm
  \code[basicstyle=\ttfamily\scriptsize]{inc_borrowed.py}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{A controlled CDKM adder at $3n$ Toffolis, not $4n$}
  \begin{columns}[c]
    \begin{column}{0.44\textwidth}
      \centering
      \begin{tikzpicture}[x=10mm,y=7mm]
        \foreach \y/\l in {3/ctrl,2/c,1/b,0/a} {\node[lab] at (0,\y) {$\l$}; \draw[wire] (0,\y) -- (4.7,\y);}
        \draw[thick,color=body] (1,2) -- (1,0); \node[dot] at (1,2) {}; \node[dot] at (1,1) {}; \oplusat{1}{0}
        \draw[thick,color=cverm] (2,3) -- (2,1); \node[dot,fill=cverm] at (2,3) {}; \node[dot] at (2,2) {}; \oplusat{2}{1}
        \draw[thick,color=body] (3,0) -- (3,2); \node[dot] at (3,0) {}; \oplusat{3}{2}
        \draw[thick,color=body] (4,0) -- (4,1); \node[dot] at (4,0) {}; \oplusat{4}{1}
        \node[note] at (1,-0.75) {$a{=}x$}; \node[note,color=cverm] at (2,-0.75) {sum};
        \node[note] at (3,-0.75) {$c{=}c_i$}; \node[note] at (4,-0.75) {$b$ done};
      \end{tikzpicture}\par
      \vskip1mm
      {\scriptsize the controlled UMA, after an \emph{uncontrolled} MAJ}
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.52\textwidth}
      \small The package documented the textbook $3n$, but put the control on every MAJ and every UMA: $4n$.
      \vskip2mm
      Compute the carries \textbf{unconditionally} (one Toffoli a bit), and unwind them with a UMA that writes the sum bit only under the control (two). With the control off, $b$ returns to $y$.
      \vskip2mm
      {\color{cverm}\bfseries One of the lean cells corrected the package.}
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}[fragile]{The controlled CDKM adder, in code}
  \code[basicstyle=\ttfamily\scriptsize]{cdkm_cadd.py}
  \vskip2mm
  {\small On entry to \texttt{\_cuma}: $b=y\oplus x$, $c=c_i\oplus x$, $a=c_{i+1}$. The carries ripple up through plain MAJs and come down through controlled UMAs.}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{Below 1\,000 qubits: share the lanes, vent a register}
  \begin{columns}[T]
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \blocktitle{Luo's extended Euclid}
      The remainders and cofactors share lanes, so the record disappears --- but each quotient bit costs a shifted comparison: $30\times$ the Toffolis for 139 qubits.
    \end{column}
    \begin{column}{0.47\textwidth}
      \blocktitle{Venting a register}
      Once $z=y/x$ exists, $y$ is a function of the others: it is $X$-measured and reused as the bank of the backward Euclid. The phase $(-1)^{b\cdot y}$ is cancelled by recomputing $y=xz$ around a $Z^b$.
    \end{column}
  \end{columns}
  \begin{takeaway}\texttt{ec\_mbu.vent} and \texttt{zfix}: the tests check that the phase cancels --- and that without the $Z^b$ it does not.\end{takeaway}
\end{frame}

\begin{frame}{The register-shared Euclidean algorithm (Luo et al.)}

\begin{frame}[fragile]{Three field registers, in code}
  \code[basicstyle=\ttfamily\fontsize{7.3}{8.4}\selectfont]{div3.py}
  {\small Without the $Z^b$ the same circuit fails the phase check: the check can see what the vent does.}
